2. RECOGNITION 
A. TSD Mission Moment – Early Childhood Center 
As t his evening’s Mission Moment , we would like to take th e opportunity to 
recognize the group of individuals who have been dedicating  their time and 
energy into developing a world -class foundation for a comprehensive pre -
kindergarten education experience for our youngest learners.  Kris Griffor, 
Assistant Superintendent of Elementary Instruction and Erin Keyser, Director of 
Early Childhood Programs will be sharing a brief video with the Board.   
RECOMMENDED ACTION: Invite Kris Griffor and Erin Keyser up to share 
     the video. 
3
     2. B.   12.18.18 
2.   RECOGNITION  
B.  12 Years of Service to the Troy Board of Education -  
Todd Miletti 
This evening, we would like to take a moment to recognize one of our own 
Board members, Todd Miletti, who is retiring from the Troy Board of Education 
and this evening is his final meeting. Todd is leaving us after selflessly giving 
12 years of service to our students, staff and families.  
Aside from being an elected position, m ost of you know that serving on the 
Board of Education is also a volunteer position, and it requires a great deal of 
time, effort and commitment. Mr. Miletti has been an  exemplary trustee and 
has done a remarkable service to past and future generations of Troy 
students. We know this is not goodbye, and he will no doubt be active in the 
greater Troy community…. but as for this Board, will miss him. 
Recommended Action:  Present certificate. 
4
  5.      12.18.18

BOARD MEETING   MINUTES NOVEMBER 20, 2018        
SERVICES BUILDING  
President Schmidt called the meeting to order at 7:03 p.m. 
In addition to Mr. Schmidt, present were Board members 
Fleming, Gottlieb, Hammond, Hauff, and Philippart.  Mr. 
Miletti was absent.  three high school student representatives 
were also in attendance. Also present were Superintendent 
Machesky; administrators  Griffor, Mueller, Harris, Jansen, 
MacDonald-Linford, and West; and approximately 40 
additional members of the audience.  
RECOGNITION 
TSD Mission Moment –  Troy 
High’s “Counseling Colts Care” 
counseling team for their investment in the Class of 2022.   
Jesse Allgeier, Anne Young and Tommy Keegan were invited 
up to share a bit about their new program.  The counselors 
have been meeting with every 9th grade student during the 
school day to help the newest Colts feel welcom e and at 
home at Troy High School.  The students join in group 
discussions and activities with the counselors.  The 
counselors want students to know that when they leave for 
the day, they see them as a caring team –  eager to help 
them for any reason at any  time during their journey 
through high school. A brief video was shared with the 
Board highlighting some of their interactions with the 
students.     
TSD Mission Moment –  Troy 
College & Career High School 
School shared the new initiatives they have been doing to 
connect with their community.  They shared a brief video 
11
Initiative 
Community Connection  
This evening, the Board recognized the Troy High 
Teachers and students from Troy College and Career High 
highlighting a Trunk or Treat event they held in the community 
as well as  a book drive where they collected and delivered 
more than 2,000 books for students in Pontiac Schools.  They 
are also partnering with Oakmont Senior Living Community 
where student visit and help with iPads and technology 
devices, bingo calling, and perform skits.  Many of the 
residents don’t have family and enjoy spending time with the 
TCCHS students.  
Planning be added to this evening’s agenda under “Other”.

BP 23 - Contract Award Recommendations 
Dear Mr. West: 
Proposals were received on November 14, 2018 for Bid Package 23.  The scope of work involves 
mechanical & electrical upgrades, including replacement of mechanical room equipment, unit ventilators, 
fire alarm, emergency lighting and door/window replacements at Troy Union and Schroeder Elementary 
Schools. Smith Middle Schools work includes a kitchen renovation, window replacements, lighting 
upgrades and mechanical unit upgrades.  International Academy East will receive a conversion of the pool 
space into classroom area as well as a new office addition for the Troy Center for Transition program.  
Troy High School will receive a renovation of the wood shop and auto shop areas.  Kitchen equipment for 
Barnard, Bemis and Hamilton is also included.    The low bidder for flooring withdrew their bid due to 
missing scope.  The second low bidder is included in the recommendations. A summary of the bids 
received is attached.  Post bid interviews are complete.  Bid pricing received including selected alternates 
is within the bond budgets assigned for each building.

10. OTHER 
A.  CONSIDERATION: Naming of Troy School District Early 
Childhood Center 
In December 2017, the Troy School Board of Education passed a 
resolution to proceed with the construction of an early childhood center 
facility.  While construction is ongoing it would be appropriate to formally 
name the facility at this time.   
 RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Accept the resolution below. 
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
 Moved by:   
 Supported by: 
WHEREAS, the Troy School District’s mission is to ensure learning for all members 
of the school community; and 
WHEREAS, the Troy School Board of Education has adopted the District’s 
strategic plan focusing their work on four characteristics of world class; and 
WHEREAS, one of the four characteristics is to Invest in the Learning of Pre -
Kindergarten Students through increasing access to a high quality, literacy -rich 
preschool experience; and 
WHEREAS, to increase access for a rich preschool experience, the Troy School 
District Board of Education approved the construction of a new Early Childhood 
Learning Center in December of 2017; and   
WHEREAS, the building is scheduled to open in the fall of 2019 and Board Policy 
6000.09 states the selection of future school names will be left to the discretion of 
the Board, 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED THAT :  The Troy School District Board 
of Education hereby names the new building the Troy School District Preschool. 
 Yes: 
 No: 
155
                  10. B.  12.18.18 
10.  OTHER 
 B. CONSIDERATION:      2019 MASB Board of Directors Nomination 
  This year, seven seats on the MAS B Board of Directors are up for 
election.  The MASB  Board of Dire ctors is comprised of 19 members : 16 
directors elected from the eight regions (two elected from each) and one 
director each from Groups V, VI an d VII.  Directors are elected by active 
member school boards and each year several sea ts are up for election. 
As approved by the 2018 Delegate Assembly, b eginning in 2019 and in 
each subsequent year, the MASB President will nominate a member to 
serve as an at -large director, subject to Board approval. Beginning in 
2021, the Board of Directors will consist of 22 members, including three 
at-large directors.   Article VIII, Section 7 of the MASB Bylaws provides 
that a board's nomination of candidates for the MASB B oard of Directors 
must be forwarded to the MASB Executive Director by Wednesday, 
January 9, 2019. 
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Adopt the following resolution. 
   Moved by  
   Supported by  
Be It Therefore Resolved  that the Troy Board of Education hereby nominates 
Steve Gottlieb, who has agreed to be a candid ate and meets the qualifi cations as 
approved by the 2018 Delegate Assembly to run for a seat on the 2019 MASB Board 
of Directors. 
Yes:  
No:  
I certify that the above resolution was adopted at the Troy Board of Education 
meeting on Tuesday, December 18, 2018. 
      Gary N. Hauff 
      Secretary, Troy Board of Education
